\varphi^{\mathrm{m}}_{\Pi({\bm{\sigma}}),{\bm{\sigma}}}(a)=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}{{\bm{\tau}}\preceq{\bm{\sigma}}}\end{subarray}}\kappa^{\mathrm{m}}_{\Pi\left({\bm{\tau}}\right),{\bm{\tau}}}(a)\;,

(6.42)

that is one recovers the formula from the pure
case with
D
indices with canonical pairing
\eta=\mathrm{id}
(
6.19
), since for any
{\bm{\tau}}\preceq{\bm{\sigma}}
,
\Pi({\bm{\tau}})
and
\Pi_{\mathrm{p}}({\bm{\tau}})
coincide. Thus for the purely-connected melonic invariants, which are first order in both situations, nothing distinguishes a pure distribution with
D
indices with Gaussian scaling function
r({\bm{\sigma}})
from a mixed distribution with scaling function
r_{W}({\bm{\sigma}})=r({\bm{\sigma}},\mathrm{id})
. The difference between the two comes from the fact that there are more first order invariants in the mixed case, namely those which are connected but not purely connected (Fig.
7
)

Observe that there are invariants for which

{\bm{\tau}}\eta^{-1}\preceq{\bm{\sigma}}\eta^{-1}
forces
{\bm{\tau}}={\bm{\sigma}}
. This is for instance the case for
{\bm{\sigma}}
melonic with all
\sigma_{c}
equal. For such invariants the asymptotic moment equals the free cumulant, and it is additive. This phenomenon is specific to the tensor case and does not arise for matrices.

If
\kappa^{\mathrm{m}}_{\bm{\tau}}(a)=1
for the first order
{\bm{\tau}}
(connected and
({\bm{\tau}},\mathrm{id})
melonic) and we pick a connected melonic invariant
{\bm{\sigma}}\in S_{n}^{D}
with
\eta=\mathrm{id}
(in which case
{\bm{\sigma}}
is purely connected) then
\varphi^{\mathrm{m}}_{{\bm{\sigma}}}(a)
is given by the same product of Catalan numbers as in (
6.21
).

Higher orders.

Prop.
6.4
which deals with the higher order in the pure case also generalizes with obvious modifications to the mixed one. In particular, for connected
{\bm{\sigma}}
,
\mathcal{K}^{\mathrm{m}}_{\bm{\sigma}}[A]
scales as:

\mathcal{K}^{\mathrm{m}}_{\bm{\sigma}}[A]_{\lvert_{K_{\mathrm{m}}({\bm{\sigma}})=1}}\asymp N^{r_{W}({\bm{\sigma}})-nD}\;,

(6.43)

which is consistent with (
3.27
) for
D=1
.

The mixed and Wishart point of vue on the pure case.

For the genuine Wishart like case, we have the following.

Proposition 6.10
.

Let
A_{i^{1}\ldots i^{D};j^{1}\ldots j^{D}}=\sum_{k=1}^{N}T_{i^{1}\ldots i^{D}k}\bar{T}_{j^{1}\ldots j^{D}k}
,
D\geq 2
, for some pure random tensor
T,\bar{T}
with
D+1
indices with Gaussian scaling (
6.10
). Let
{\bm{\sigma}}\in S_{n}^{D}
be such that
({\bm{\sigma}},\mathrm{id})
is melonic with canonical pairing
\eta
. Then the pure free cumulants of
T,\bar{T}
and the mixed free cumulants of
A
are related by:

\kappa_{\Pi_{\mathrm{p}}({\bm{\sigma}},\mathrm{id}),({\bm{\sigma}},\mathrm{id})}(t,\bar{t})=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}{\nu\in S_{n}}\\
{\nu\eta^{-1}\preceq\eta^{-1}}\end{subarray}}\kappa^{\mathrm{m}}_{\Pi\left({\bm{\sigma}}\nu^{-1}\right),{\bm{\sigma}}\nu^{-1}}(a)\;\mathsf{M}(\nu)\;,

(6.44)

where
\nu\in S_{n}
encodes a change of the thick edges representing the tensors
A
.
28
28

28

If we change variable to
{\bm{\sigma}}^{\prime}={\bm{\sigma}}\nu^{-1}
corresponding to a change of labeling of the white vertices, the identity after the change of variables represents the thick edges, but before this change of labeling, the thick edges are represented by the permutation
\nu
.

Conversely:

\kappa^{\mathrm{m}}_{\Pi\left({\bm{\sigma}}\right),{\bm{\sigma}}}(a)=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}{\nu\in S_{n}}\\
{\nu\eta^{-1}\preceq\eta^{-1}}\end{subarray}}\kappa_{\Pi_{\mathrm{p}}({\bm{\sigma}},\nu),({\bm{\sigma}},\nu)}(t,\bar{t})\;.

(6.45)

If
{\bm{\sigma}}
is purely connected,
\eta=\mathrm{id}
and

\kappa_{({\bm{\sigma}},\mathrm{id})}(t,\bar{t})=\kappa^{\mathrm{m}}_{{\bm{\sigma}}}(a)
.

Proof.

See Section
B.6
.
∎

Figure 10:

) for
{\bm{\sigma}}
as on the upper left of Fig.

: the new permutation
\nu
represents a modification of the thick edges (see Footnote

).

In particular, if
T
is a pure Gaussian with
D+1
indices,
D\geq 2
, and
A
is as in (

), we have from (

) that

\kappa_{\Pi_{\mathrm{p}}({\bm{\sigma}},\nu),({\bm{\sigma}},\nu)}(t,\bar{t})=\delta_{({\bm{\sigma}},\nu),(\eta,\ldots,\eta)}
where
\eta
is the canonical pairing of
({\bm{\sigma}},\mathrm{id})
.
Inserting this into (
6.45
) for
{\bm{\sigma}}
connected with
({\bm{\sigma}},\mathrm{id})
melonic, we see that the right-hand side is zero unless
{\bm{\sigma}}=(\eta,\ldots,\eta)\in S_{n}^{D}
and
\nu=\eta
. For
{\bm{\sigma}}
to be connected,
\eta
must therefore be a cycle of length
n
. We find for
{\bm{\sigma}}
connected with
({\bm{\sigma}},\mathrm{id})
melonic that:

\kappa^{\mathrm{m}}_{{\bm{\sigma}}}(w)=\delta_{{\bm{\sigma}},(\eta,\ldots,\eta)},

(6.46)

where
\eta
is a cycle of length
n
, and more generally if
{\bm{\sigma}}
is not necessarily connected that
\kappa^{\mathrm{m}}_{\Pi({\bm{\sigma}}),{\bm{\sigma}}}(w)=\delta_{{\bm{\sigma}},(\eta,\ldots,\eta)}
, without condition on
\eta
. This is different from the case of a pure complex Gaussian with two indices (a random matrix) (
6.5
), but it directly generalize the result for the
D=1
Wishart matrix (
6.9
).
However, for
D\geq 2
, applying (
6.41
) leads for
{\bm{\sigma}}
connected with
({\bm{\sigma}},\mathrm{id})
melonic to:

\varphi^{\mathrm{m}}_{{\bm{\sigma}}}(w)=1,

(6.47)

as expected, since again
{\bm{\tau}}=(\eta,\ldots,\eta)
is the only non-vanishing term in the sum (

).

7
Asymptotic tensor freeness

7.1
Matrix freeness

Let us first review the notion of freeness adapted to unitarily invariant random matrices
A,B\in\mathcal{M}_{N}(\mathbb{C})

[
60
,
62
]
.
If
M=(M_{1},\ldots,M_{n})\in\{A,B\}^{n}
and defining
\kappa_{n}(m_{1},\ldots,m_{n})
where
m_{i}\in\{a,b\}
,
A,B
are asymptotically free if and only if for all
n\geq 2
:

\kappa_{n}(m_{1},\ldots,m_{n})=0\;,

(7.1)

whenever there exist
1\leq i<j\leq n
such that
m_{i}\neq m_{j}
. For matrices, one can make sense of
a,b
as being non-commutative random variables, that is, elements of a non-commutative probability space defined as an algebra
\mathcal{A}
with a unit element
1
, together with a linear functional
\varphi:\mathcal{A}\rightarrow\mathbb{C}
, that maps the unit element to 1. The family
\varphi(a^{n})
,
n\geq 1
are the moments of
a
. For a single variable
a
this relates for
n\geq 1
to
\varphi_{n}
defined before as
\varphi(a^{n})=\varphi_{n}(a)
where
a^{n}\in\mathcal{A}
.

\varphi
corresponds asymptotically to “
\frac{1}{N}\mathbb{E}[{\mathrm{Tr}}(\cdot)]
”.
A random matrix
A
converges in distribution towards
a
when
N\rightarrow\infty
if for all
n\in\mathbb{N}^{\star}
,
\frac{1}{N}\mathbb{E}[{\mathrm{Tr}}(A^{n})]\rightarrow\varphi(a^{n})
, and the statement above for the
asymptotic freeness
of
A,B
directly refers to the
freeness
of the non-commutative random variables
a
and
b
.

Freeness is equivalent to the vanishing of mixed centered moments (the original formulation
[
60
]
), that is, for any
n\geq 2
, for any
(m_{1},\ldots,m_{n})
with
m_{i}\in\{a,b\}
(or some finite fixed set of elements of
\mathcal{A}
), and any
g_{i}
in the subalgebra generated by 1 and
m_{i}
:

\varphi\bigl(g_{1}\cdots g_{n}\bigr)=0,

(7.2)

whenever
\varphi(g_{i})=0
for all
g_{i}
, and
g_{1}\cdots g_{n}
are
almost alternating
29
29

29

The elements
g_{1}\cdots g_{n}
are
strictly
alternating if
g_{1}
and
g_{n}
belong to different subalgebras.
, that is,
g_{i}
and
g_{i+1}
belong to two different subalgebras. Since the variables can be centered, for two variables
a,b
, this is equivalent to requiring that for all
k\geq 1
and all
n_{1},\ldots,n_{k}\geq 1
,
m_{1}\geq 1
, if
k\geq 2

m_{k}\geq 0
, and if
k\geq 3

m_{2}\ldots,m_{k-1}\geq 1
:

\varphi\Bigl(\bigl(a^{n_{1}}-\varphi(a^{n_{1}})1\bigr)\bigl(b^{m_{1}}-\varphi(b^{m_{1}})1\bigr)\cdots\bigl(a^{n_{k}}-\varphi(a^{n_{k}})1\bigr)\bigl(b^{m_{k}}-\varphi(b^{m_{k}})1\bigr)\Bigr)=0.

(7.3)

7.2
Asymptotic tensor freeness at the level of free cumulants

Having defined free cumulants of pure and mixed random tensors, we define asymptotic (first order) tensor freeness of a collection of random tensors as the vanishing of
\kappa_{{\bm{\sigma}}}
involving different elements
30
30

30

Called mixed cumulants, not to be confused with the term “mixed” for a tensor, which comes from the quantum information interpretation of
LU
-invariant distributions as distributions over quantum states.
for any
first order
{\bm{\sigma}}
. More precisely:

•

For mixed tensors

A,B,\ldots
that scale like the Wishart tensor (
6.37
) and (
6.38
),
D\geq 2
, the first order consists of the
connected

{\bm{\sigma}}\in S_{n}^{D}
,
n\geq 1
, such that
\omega({\bm{\sigma}},\mathrm{id})=0
. This constrains the thick edges as follows: in the graph
{\bm{\sigma}}
, any cycle of alternated thick edges and canonical pairs that involves more than one thick edge must be separating (Fig.
7
).
A,B,\ldots
are asymptotically free if for any such
{\bm{\sigma}}
and any
\vec{m}=(m_{1},\ldots,m_{n})
with
m_{i}\in\{a,b,\ldots\}
:

\kappa^{\mathrm{m}}_{{\bm{\sigma}}}(\vec{m})=0,

(7.4)

whenever
m_{i}\neq m_{j}
for some
i\neq j
.

•

For pure tensors
,
(T_{a},\bar{T}_{a})
,
(T_{b},\bar{T}_{b})

\ldots
that scale like a complex Gaussian (
6.10
) and (
6.15
),
D\geq 3
, the first order consists of the
purely connected and melonic
{\bm{\sigma}}\in S_{n}^{D}
,
n\geq 1
. We require that
{\bm{\sigma}}
be labeled such that its canonical pairing
\eta
is the identity and consider for
1\leq i\leq n
,
\vec{X}=(X_{1},\dots X_{n})
and
\vec{X^{\prime}}=(X^{\prime}_{\bar{1}},\dots X^{\prime}_{\bar{n}})
with
X_{i}\in\{T_{a},T_{b}\ldots\}
and
X^{\prime}_{\bar{s}}\in\{\bar{T}_{a},\bar{T}_{b}\ldots\}
. Note that we allow
X^{\prime}_{\bar{s}}\neq\bar{X}_{s}
, that is, one could have
X_{s}=T_{a}
and
X^{\prime}_{\bar{s}}=\bar{T}_{b}
. In that case,
(T_{a},\bar{T}_{a})
,
(T_{b},\bar{T}_{b})

\ldots
are asymptotically free if:

\kappa_{{\bm{\sigma}}}(\vec{x},\vec{x^{\prime}})=0,

(7.5)

whenever
x_{i}\neq x_{j}
or
x_{\bar{i}}^{\prime}\neq x_{\bar{j}}^{\prime}
, or
\overline{x_{i}}\neq x_{\bar{j}}^{\prime}
for some
i,j
.

In the pure case, if there exists
\eta^{-1}
such that for each
s
,
X_{\overline{\eta^{-1}(s)}}^{\prime}=\overline{X_{s}}
, then by changing the labeling of the white vertices one may replace
{\bm{\sigma}}
by
{\bm{\sigma}}\eta
, setting
X_{\bar{s}}^{\prime}=\overline{X_{s}}
with now
\eta
defining the canonical pairing. After this change of labeling, the black and white vertices can be seen as representing the inputs and the outputs of the same tensor
X_{s}\otimes\overline{X_{s}}
, and are linked by a thick edge encoded by the permutation
\mathrm{id}
. For
\vec{X},\vec{X}^{\prime}
satisfying this, the vanishing condition for purely connected
{\bm{\sigma}}
with canonical pairing the identity (i.e. with
\bar{\omega}({\bm{\sigma}};\mathrm{id})=0
) must be considered in both the pure and mixed cases. On the other hand, the vanishing condition in the pure case for
\vec{X},\vec{X}^{\prime}
which do not satisfy this condition must be considered
only in the pure case
, while the vanishing condition for
{\bm{\sigma}}
such that
\omega({\bm{\sigma}},\mathrm{id})=0
, connected but not purely, must be considered
only in the mixed case
.

7.3
Paired tensors

In order to state the definition of matrix freeness at the level of moments (Sec.
7.1
), it was necessary to consider elements in the subalgebras
\mathcal{A}[a,1]
generated by
a
and
1
.
We let
\mathcal{G}[A]=\{A^{n}\}_{n\geq 1}
: the algebra
\mathcal{A}[A,\mathbbm{1}]
generated by
A
and
\mathbbm{1}
can also be seen as the set of linear combinations of elements of
\{\mathbbm{1}\}\cup\mathcal{G}[A]
. In the tensor case, stating the moments version of tensor freeness will require extending the notions of tensors and trace-invariants. We will introduce here some sets which will generalize the roles of
\mathcal{G}[A]
, of
\mathcal{A}[A,\mathbbm{1}]
, and then of
\mathcal{A}[a,1]
in Sec.
8
.

Paired tensors.

A
paired tensor
is a tensor with components
P_{\{i_{c,r},j_{c,r}\}}\in\mathbb{C}
, where
1\leq c\leq D
and
r\in\{0,\ldots,k_{c}\}
,
1\leq i_{c,r},j_{c,r}\leq N
, and where
k_{c}\geq 0
and
\sum_{c=1}^{D}k_{c}\geq 1
. There may therefore be no index of color
c
or there can be several of the same color
c
, in which case the second index
r
partitions the inputs and outputs of the same color in pairs. We refer to
r
as the
shade
: a pair of outputs and inputs
(i_{c,r},j_{c,r})
has color
c
and shade
r
. The inputs or outputs of the same color
c
carrying different shades
r
are distinguishable.
Tensors
A
or
T\otimes\bar{T}
considered earlier in the text are paired tensors.
If
\mathcal{D}=\sum_{c}k_{c}
, the identity
\mathbbm{1}^{\otimes\mathcal{D}}
of
\mathcal{M}_{N}(\mathbb{C})^{\otimes\mathcal{D}}
can be viewed as a paired tensor:

{\mathbbm{1}^{\otimes\mathcal{D}}}_{\{i_{c,r},j_{c,r}\}}=\prod_{c=1}^{D}\prod_{r=1}^{k_{c}}\delta_{i_{c,r},j_{c,r}}.

Trace-invariants of paired tensors.

We generalize the notion of trace-invariants for paired tensors. Given
n
labeled paired tensors
P_{1},\ldots,P_{n}
, if we represent these tensors by thick edges linking a black and a white vertex, with
k_{c}(i)
distinguishable half-edges of color
c
respectively attached to the inputs and the outputs of the tensor
P_{i}
for each color
1\leq c\leq D
, then the trace-invariants of these paired tensors are encoded by colored graphs
\mathsf{g}
with thick edges representing the tensors, and edges of color
c
representing the index summations. A vertex may now have none or several incident edges for each color
c
. We use the notation
{\mathrm{Tr}}_{\mathsf{g}}(\vec{P})
. See the example in Fig.
11
.

We cannot simply use a single permutation per color as before in order to encode labeled invariants, since here a tensor may now have different pairs of indices of the same color.
However, in the graph there are cycles which go from the output of a tensor which has subscript
(c,r_{1})
, through an edge of color
c
, to the intput of a tensor which has subscript
(c,r_{2})
, to the output of the same tensor to which it is paired, through an edge of color
c
, etc. If the paired tensors are labeled from 1 to
n
, we thus obtain a set of
cycles

\{\gamma_{c,b}\}
, each of the form
\bigl([k_{1},r_{1}]\cdots[k_{q},r_{q}]\bigr)
where
1\leq k_{1},\ldots,k_{q}\leq n
are the labels of the thick edges (paired tensors) encountered in the cycle (all distinct), and
r_{s}
is the shade of the pair of output and input of color
c
of the paired tensor number
k_{s}
which belongs to the cycle under consideration.
The index
b
labels the different cycles that have the same color
c
.
Starting from the collection of labeled paired tensors, one can reconstruct all the colored edges from this data: an output of color
c
and shade
r
of a given tensor
k
appears in only one cycle
\gamma_{c,b_{0}}
of the
\{\gamma_{c,b}\}
, and if
\gamma_{c,b_{0}}\bigl([k,r])=[k^{\prime},r^{\prime}]
, an edge of color
c
is added between the output of color
c
, shade
r
of
k
and the input of color
c
, shade
r^{\prime}
of
k^{\prime}
.

Figure 11:
Melonic graph of four paired tensors, represented as thick edges with labels between brackets. The color is indicated on the edges, and the shade is indicated in magenta in the only case where it is needed (
k_{c}>1
).
The labeled invariant is encoded by the cycles
\gamma_{1,1}=\bigl([2,1][3,2][4,1]\bigr)
,
\gamma_{1,2}=\bigl([3,1]\bigr)
,
\gamma_{2,1}=\bigl([1,1][2,1]\bigr)
,
\gamma_{2,2}=\bigl([4,1]\bigr)
and
\gamma_{3,1}=\bigl([4,1]\bigr)
.

Melonic invariants of paired tensors.

We can generalize for paired tensors the recursive construction that defines melonic graphs and invariants (Sec.
5.2
). For our use here, it is enough to define them with thick edges on the canonical pairs (so in the following, “melonic graphs
of paired tensors
” always have thick edges on the canonical pairs). The definition is the following:

•

A melonic invariant with one paired tensor
P
is always denoted as
\mathbf{id}_{1}
(the number of indices is not made explicit unless there is a possible confusion). It corresponds to a graph with two vertices linked by one thick edge and a number of colored edges. One has:

{\mathrm{Tr}}_{\mathbf{id}_{1}}(P)={\mathrm{Tr}}(P)={\sum}_{\{i_{c,r}\}_{c,r}}P_{\{i_{c,r},i_{c,r}\}}

(7.6)

•

A
connected
melonic invariant with more than one paired tensor always has a tensor with
\mathcal{D}\geq 1
inputs, of which
\mathcal{D}-1
are summed with their paired outputs. In the colored graph, it corresponds to a thick edge with a number of edges (possibly zero) with colors in
1,\ldots,D
linking the same two vertices, with the exception of one pair of an input and output of the same shade. Removing this tensor and reconnecting the two pending half-edges as in Fig.
5
, one gets a smaller melonic graph.
31
31

31

So for a melonic invariant of paired tensors, a paired tensor with a single input and output (such as the one labeled
[1]
in Fig.
11
) always corresponds to a canonical pair.
An example is shown in Fig.
11
.

Consider a connected melonic invariant with graph
\mathsf{g}
as just defined, but for some regular (labeled) tensors
M_{1},\ldots M_{n}
(so the vertices have exactly one incident edge of color
c
for each
1\leq c\leq D
) with
M_{i}\in\{A,B\ldots\}
in the mixed case or
M_{i}=X_{i}\otimes X_{\bar{i}}^{\prime}
with
X_{i}\in\{T_{a},T_{b}\ldots\}
and
X_{\bar{i}}^{\prime}\in\{\bar{T}_{a},\bar{T}_{b}\ldots\}
in the pure case. Then this invariant is a purely connected melonic invariant
{\bm{\sigma}}
in the usual sense, and the canonical pairing is the identity since by construction, the black and white vertices of the canonical pairs are linked by the thick edges.

To generate all first order invariants in the mixed case, one may consider
q
paired tensors
P^{\ell}
,
1\leq\ell\leq q
, of the form:

P^{\ell}_{\{i_{c,r},j_{c,r}\}_{\begin{subarray}{c}{1\leq c\leq D}\\
{1\leq r\leq k_{\ell}}\end{subarray}}}=(M^{\ell}_{1})_{i_{1,1},\ldots i_{D,1};j_{1,2},\ldots j_{D,2}}(M^{\ell}_{2})_{i_{1,2},\ldots i_{D,2};j_{1,3},\ldots j_{D,3}}\ \cdots\ (M^{\ell}_{k_{\ell}})_{i_{1,k_{\ell}},\ldots i_{D,k_{\ell}};j_{1,1},\ldots j_{D,1}}.

(7.7)

A melonic invariants of
q
paired tensors
P^{1},\ldots P^{q}
of this kind is a trace invariant
{\mathrm{Tr}}_{\bm{\sigma}}[\{M^{\ell}_{i}\}]
for
{\bm{\sigma}}\in S_{n}^{D}
with
n=\sum_{\ell=1}^{q}k_{\ell}
, connected and such that
({\bm{\sigma}},\mathrm{id})
is melonic. The canonical pairing of
({\bm{\sigma}},\mathrm{id})
is given by the pairing of inputs and outputs of (
7.7
). All the invariants of this kind can be generated this way, since inserting a tensor of the form (
7.7
) reproduces a cycle alternating thick edges and canonical pairs which is separating for
k_{\ell}>1
.

Paired tensors generated by first-order invariants.

To a tensor
A
(mixed or pure
A=T_{a}\otimes\bar{T}_{a}
), we associate a family of paired tensors as follows. Take any first order invariant
{\bm{\sigma}}
(in the mixed case,
{\bm{\sigma}}
is connected and
({\bm{\sigma}},\mathrm{id})
is melonic, and in the pure case, with the convention of Sec.
7.2
,
{\bm{\sigma}}
is purely connected melonic with canonical pairing the identity), and consider the cycles in the graph which alternate edges of color
c
and canonical pairs. For each such cycle, choose an edge and split it open. Each edge deletion removes in the corresponding trace-invariant a summation between the output and the input of two (non-necessarily distinct) tensors, and these indices are now
free indices
in the sense that they are not summed, and are
paired
(the corresponding half-edges are linked by a path of edges of color
c
for some
c
and canonical pairs). If
E
is the set of edges which have been split open (that is,
E
is a set of pairs of the form
(i,\sigma_{c}(i))
for which the summation is not carried in the definition of the trace-invariant (
4.2
)), we denote by
{\mathrm{Tr}}_{{\bm{\sigma}}_{\setminus E}}(A)
the resulting “partial trace-invariant”. It is a paired tensor with
k_{c}
paired inputs and outputs for each color
c
, and if there are
n
regular tensors
A
, the number of inputs of
{\mathrm{Tr}}_{{\bm{\sigma}}_{\setminus E}}(A)
is easily seen to be:

\mathcal{D}=\sum_{c=1}^{D}k_{c}=n(D-1)+K,

(7.8)

where
K
is the number of connected components. If the canonical pairing of
{\bm{\sigma}}
coincides with the thick edges, the
(D+1)
-colored graph
{\bm{\sigma}}_{\setminus E}
for which the thick edges are represented is connected and it is a tree. The graph is not connected if the canonical pairing of
{\bm{\sigma}}
differs from the thick edges (a situation which occurs only in the mixed case)
32
32

32

K
is one plus the sum over cycles alternating thick edges and canonical pairs of the number of thick edges in the cycle minus one.
, and each connected component is a tree. In the latter case, there are inputs paired with outputs from a different connected component.

The simplest example is the tensor
A
itself (in the pure case
A=T_{a}\otimes\bar{T}_{a}
). In the mixed case, paired tensors of the form (
7.7
) with all
M_{a}^{\ell}
equal to
A
are generated this way.
See Fig.
12
for more generic examples.

Figure 12:
Examples of paired tensors generated by the first order invariants of Fig.
7
(the left one - with nine inputs - may be considered both for the pure and mixed cases, and the one on the right - with thirteen inputs - for the mixed case only). The arrows indicate the paired indices.

We denote by
\mathcal{G}^{\mathrm{m}}_{D,\mathcal{D}}[A]
and by
\mathcal{G}^{\mathrm{p}}_{D,\mathcal{D}}[T,\bar{T}]
the sets of paired tensors with
\mathcal{D}
inputs generated through this procedure by
A
for the mixed first order invariants or by
T,\bar{T}
(though the choice
A=T\otimes\bar{T}
) for the pure first order invariants. These sets generalize the set
\mathcal{G}[A]=\mathcal{G}^{\mathrm{m}}_{1,1}[A]=\{A^{n}\}_{n\geq 1}
defined above
33
33

33

Indeed for the mixed
D=1
case, a first order trace-invariant corresponds graphically to a cycle of length
n\geq 1
consisting alternatively of edges of color 1 and thick edges, and splitting any edge open in this cycle, one obtains
A^{n}
, with
{\mathcal{D}}=1
input.
.
One may also consider first order trace-invariants of some possibly different tensors: In the mixed case,
\vec{M}
then takes value in a fixed set
S=\{A,B\ldots\}
, while in the pure case,
\vec{X}
and
\vec{X}^{\prime}
respectively take value in some sets
\Theta=\{T_{a},T_{b}\ldots\}
and
\bar{\Theta}=\{\bar{T}_{a},\bar{T}_{b}\ldots\}
.
34
34

34

In this case, as in Sec.
7.2
, we keep the thick edges on the canonical pairs. Each thick edge represents
X_{s}\otimes X^{\prime}_{\bar{s}}
, but we allow
X^{\prime}_{\bar{s}}\neq\bar{X}_{s}
.
We then use the notations
\mathcal{G}^{\mathrm{m}}_{D,\mathcal{D}}[S]
and
\mathcal{G}^{\mathrm{p}}_{D,\mathcal{D}}[\Theta,\bar{\Theta}]
for the resulting sets of paired tensors (for the
D=1
mixed case, this is the set of matrices
AB^{3}A^{2}C\cdots
formed by multiplying words of matrices in the set
S
). We let
\mathcal{G}^{\mathrm{m}}_{D}=\bigcup_{{\mathcal{D}}\geq 1}\mathcal{G}^{\mathrm{m}}_{D,\mathcal{D}}
and
\mathcal{G}^{\mathrm{p}}_{D}=\bigcup_{{\mathcal{D}}\geq 1}\mathcal{G}^{\mathrm{p}}_{D,\mathcal{D}}
. We say that an element of
\mathcal{G}^{\mathrm{m}}_{D}[S]
or
\mathcal{G}^{\mathrm{p}}_{D}[\Theta,\bar{\Theta}]
is
generated by

S
or
(\Theta,\bar{\Theta})
.

7.4
Grouping and ungrouping tensors

Ungrouping tensors.

Consider some paired tensors
H_{1},\ldots H_{p}
with
H_{\ell}\in\mathcal{G}^{\mathrm{m}}_{D}[S_{\ell}]
, where
S_{\ell}\subset\{A,B\ldots\}
, or
H_{\ell}\in\mathcal{G}^{\mathrm{p}}_{D}[\Theta_{\ell},\bar{\Theta}_{\ell}]
, where
\Theta_{\ell}\subset\{T_{a},T_{b}\ldots\}
and
\bar{\Theta}_{\ell}\subset\{\bar{T}_{a},\bar{T}_{b}\ldots\}
and a colored graph
\mathsf{g}
of
\vec{H}
. Assume that
\vec{H}
involves in total
n
regular tensors
M_{1},\ldots M_{n}
,
M_{i}\in\{A,B\ldots\}
or
M_{i}=X_{i}\otimes X_{\bar{i}}^{\prime}
with
X_{i}\in\{T_{a},T_{b}\ldots\}
and
X_{\bar{i}}^{\prime}\in\{\bar{T}_{a},\bar{T}_{b}\ldots\}
. One can encode the index summations represented by both the edges of
\mathsf{g}
as well as the internal edges of all the
H_{\ell}
using a
D
-tuple of permutations
{\bm{\sigma}}\in S_{n}^{D}
, and then:

{\mathrm{Tr}}_{\mathsf{g}}(\vec{H})={\mathrm{Tr}}_{\bm{\sigma}}(\vec{M}),

(7.9)

which in the pure case where
M_{i}=X_{i}\otimes X_{\bar{i}}^{\prime}
can also be written with the dedicated notation
{\mathrm{Tr}}_{\mathsf{g}}(\vec{H})={\mathrm{Tr}}_{\bm{\sigma}}(\vec{M})={\mathrm{Tr}}_{\bm{\sigma}}(\vec{X},\vec{X}^{\prime})
. See the examples of Fig.
13
.

Lemma 7.1
.

With these notations,
\mathsf{g}
is a melonic graph of paired tensors if and only if
{\bm{\sigma}}
is first order, i.e.
({\bm{\sigma}},\mathrm{id})
is connected and melonic in the mixed case, or
{\bm{\sigma}}
is purely connected with canonical pairing the identity in the pure case.

Proof.

It is equivalent to show that every cycle that alternates canonical pairs and at least two edges of color
c
for any
1\leq c\leq D
is separating (where in the case of paired tensors, canonical pairs coincide with the thick edges and the inputs and outputs are of the same shade). This holds because of the fact that the paired inputs and outputs of an element
H_{\ell}
of
\mathcal{G}^{\mathrm{m}}_{D}[S_{\ell}]
or
\mathcal{G}^{\mathrm{p}}_{D}[\Theta_{\ell},\bar{\Theta}_{\ell}]
are linked inside
H_{\ell}
by a sequence of edges of color
c
and canonical pairs.
∎

Figure 13:
Two examples of melonic graphs of paired tensors
\mathsf{g}
, together with some explicit choices of paired tensors
H_{1},\ldots H_{4}
generated by
T,\bar{T}
on the left (the white and black vertices) and by
A
on the right (the thick edges). The grey box labeled
[i]
represents the paired tensor
H_{i}
, and the blue blobs represent the canonical pairs. The edges which are not entirely included inside the grey boxes are edges of
\mathsf{g}
and the ones entirely included in the grey boxes are edges of the
H_{i}
. Considering the edges of
\mathsf{g}
as well as those internal to the paired tensors, one gets a first order invariant of the regular tensors
T,\bar{T}

in the pure case and

A

in the mixed case.

Assuming
\mathsf{g}
to be melonic and if now
E
is a set of edges of
{\mathsf{g}}
with colors in
\{1,\ldots D\}
and
with one edge per cycle that alternates edges of color
c
and paired inputs and outputs
, we use the notation
{\mathrm{Tr}}_{\mathsf{g}_{\setminus E}}(\vec{H})
for the partial trace-invariant, which has
\lvert E\rvert
inputs. From (
7.9
) and noticing that by construction there is still in
{\bm{\sigma}}
one edge of
E
per cycle alternating non-thick edges and canonical pairs:

{\mathrm{Tr}}_{\mathsf{g}_{\setminus E}}(\vec{H})={\mathrm{Tr}}_{{\bm{\sigma}}_{\setminus E}}(\vec{M}),

(7.10)

which in the mixed case is an element of
\mathcal{G}^{\mathrm{m}}_{D}[S]
with
S=\cup_{\ell}S_{\ell}
, and in the pure case is an element of
\mathcal{G}^{\mathrm{p}}_{D}[\Theta,\bar{\Theta}]
, with
\Theta=\cup_{\ell}\Theta_{\ell}
and
\bar{\Theta}=\cup_{\ell}\bar{\Theta}_{\ell}
and
{\mathrm{Tr}}_{{\bm{\sigma}}_{\setminus E}}(\vec{M})={\mathrm{Tr}}_{{\bm{\sigma}}_{\setminus E}}(\vec{X},\vec{X}^{\prime})
.
In both cases
\mathsf{g}_{\setminus E}
is a tree, and
{\mathrm{Tr}}_{\mathsf{g}_{\setminus E}}(\vec{H})
is a paired tensor,
and the result of applying to it
{\mathrm{Tr}}_{\mathbf{id}_{1}}={\mathrm{Tr}}
defined in (
7.6
) is:

{\mathrm{Tr}}\bigl({\mathrm{Tr}}_{\mathsf{g}_{\setminus E}}(\vec{H})\bigr)={\mathrm{Tr}}\bigl({\mathrm{Tr}}_{{\bm{\sigma}}_{\setminus E}}(\vec{M})\bigr)={\mathrm{Tr}}_{\mathsf{g}}(\vec{H})={\mathrm{Tr}}_{\bm{\sigma}}(\vec{M}).

(7.11)

Grouping tensors.

If now we remove the condition on
E
so that it can be
any set
of edges of color
1,\ldots D
of
\mathsf{g}
(a melonic graph of paired tensors), then the colored graph
\mathsf{g}_{\setminus E}
might no longer be connected (Fig.
14
). Letting
\vec{H}_{\lvert_{\jmath}}
be the restriction of
\vec{H}
to the set
R_{\jmath}\subset\{1,\dots p\}
of the labels of the thick edges that belong to the connected component number
\jmath
of
\mathsf{g}_{\setminus E}
, the partial trace of
\vec{H}_{\lvert_{\jmath}}
associated to the connected component number
\jmath
is a paired tensor
P_{\jmath}
. Starting from a half-edge in
\mathsf{g}_{\setminus E}
(created by splitting an edge open), and alternatively following paired tensors and pairs of inputs and outputs of the same shade, one must eventually arrive at another half-edge. Connecting these two half-edges to form an edge (the resulting edge might or might not be an edge of
\mathsf{g}
), and repeating the operation for all the half-edges of
\mathsf{g}_{\setminus E}
, one obtains a graph
\mathsf{h}
encoding an invariant of the paired tensors
\vec{H}
whose connected components
\mathsf{h}_{\jmath}
involve the same
H_{\ell}
,
\ell\in R_{j}
as the
P_{\jmath}
. To recover
P_{\jmath}
from
\mathsf{h}_{\jmath}
, one must remove some edges, at most one edge is removed for each cycle alternating paired indices and colored edges.

Figure 14:

Left: the example of Fig.
11
and a set of edges
E
(crossed). Center: splitting these edges open, one gets a collection of paired tensors
P_{\jmath}
. Right: the melonic graphs
\mathsf{h}_{\jmath}
.

In addition, since
\mathsf{g}
is a melonic graph of paired tensors that are elements of
\mathcal{G}^{\mathrm{m}}_{D}[S_{\ell}]
or
\mathcal{G}^{\mathrm{p}}_{D}[\Theta_{\ell},\bar{\Theta}_{\ell}]
, there is a
{\bm{\sigma}}\in S_{n}^{D}

first order (Lemma
7.9
), and the relevant canonical pairing
\eta
,
and a
\vec{M}
, such that
{\mathrm{Tr}}_{\mathsf{g}}(\vec{H})={\mathrm{Tr}}_{\bm{\sigma}}(\vec{M})
(in the pure case,
M_{i}=X_{i}\otimes X_{i}^{\prime}
and one can also write
{\mathrm{Tr}}_{\mathsf{g}}(\vec{H})={\mathrm{Tr}}_{\bm{\sigma}}(\vec{M})={\mathrm{Tr}}_{\bm{\sigma}}(\vec{X},\vec{X}^{\prime})
). There is also a
{\bm{\tau}}\in S_{n}^{D}
with connected components
{\bm{\tau}}_{\jmath}
satisfying
{\mathrm{Tr}}_{\mathsf{h}_{\jmath}}(\vec{H}_{\jmath})={\mathrm{Tr}}_{{\bm{\tau}}_{\jmath}}(\vec{M}_{\jmath})
with
\vec{M}_{\jmath}=\vec{M}_{\lvert_{R_{\jmath}}}
(so in the pure case,
{\mathrm{Tr}}_{\mathsf{h}_{\jmath}}(\vec{H}_{\jmath})={\mathrm{Tr}}_{{\bm{\tau}}_{\jmath}}(\vec{X}_{\jmath},\vec{X}_{\jmath}^{\prime})
), and one has
35
35

35

Indeed the cycles that alternate edges of color
c
and paired indices in
\mathsf{h}
or
\mathsf{g}
correspond to cycles that alternate edges of color
c
and canonical pairs in
{\bm{\tau}}
or
{\bm{\sigma}}
. The operation of splitting open the edges of
E
and reconnecting them to the first half-edge that follows, indeed builds a non-crossing permutation for each such cycle (it’s the smallest that leave the edges not in
E
unchanged).

{\bm{\tau}}\eta^{-1}\preceq{\bm{\sigma}}\eta^{-1}
, and therefore in the mixed case
({\bm{\tau}},\mathrm{id})
is melonic with canonical pairing
\eta
(Thm.
6.9
), and in the pure case
{\bm{\tau}}
is melonic with canonical pairing the identity (Thm.
6.3
). In both cases, the connected components
\{{\bm{\tau}}_{\jmath}\}
of
{\bm{\tau}}
are first order invariants, so from Lemma
7.9

the
\{\mathsf{h}_{\jmath}\}
are connected melonic graphs of the paired tensors
\vec{H}_{\lvert_{\jmath}}
.
If in addition
E
is such that
P_{\jmath}
is obtained from
{\bm{\tau}}_{\jmath}
(which is first order) by removing one edge per cycle alternating edges of color
1\leq c\leq D
and canonical pairs, then by definition of the generated sets:

P_{\jmath}\in\mathcal{G}^{\mathrm{m}}_{D}[S_{\jmath}]\qquad\textrm{(mixed case)},\qquad\textrm{and}\qquad P_{\jmath}\in\mathcal{G}^{\mathrm{p}}_{D}[\Theta_{\jmath},\bar{\Theta}_{\jmath}]\qquad\textrm{(pure case)}

(7.12)

where
S_{\jmath}=\cup_{\ell\in R_{j}}S_{\ell}
,
\Theta_{\jmath}=\cup_{\ell\in R_{j}}\Theta_{\ell}
and
\bar{\Theta}_{\jmath}=\cup_{\ell\in R_{j}}\bar{\Theta}_{\ell}
. Furthermore, the trace-invariants corresponding to the
\mathsf{h}_{\jmath}
satisfy:

{\mathrm{Tr}}_{\mathsf{h}_{\jmath}}(\vec{H}_{\jmath})={\mathrm{Tr}}_{\mathbf{id}_{1}}(P_{\jmath})={\mathrm{Tr}}(P_{\jmath}).

(7.13)

One can also see
{\mathrm{Tr}}_{\mathsf{g}}(\vec{H})
itself as a melonic invariant
\mathsf{k}
of the paired tensors
\vec{P}
. The edges of the set
E
considered above encode summations between the indices of the
P_{\jmath}
. Letting
\mathsf{k}
be the colored graph with one thick edge per paired tensor
P_{\jmath}
and whose colored edges are the edges of
E
, we have:

{\mathrm{Tr}}_{\mathsf{g}}(\vec{H})={\mathrm{Tr}}_{\mathsf{k}}(\vec{P}).

(7.14)

In particular, starting from
{\bm{\sigma}}\in S_{n}^{D}
purely connected with
\bar{\omega}({\bm{\sigma}};\mathrm{id})=0
and
regular
tensors
\vec{Q}\in\{A,B\ldots\}
(or the pure equivalent
Q_{i}=X_{i}\otimes X_{\bar{i}}^{\prime}
with
X_{i}\in\{T_{a},T_{b}\ldots\}
and
X_{\bar{i}}^{\prime}\in\{\bar{T}_{a},\bar{T}_{b}\ldots\}
) instead of
paired
tensors
\vec{H}
, choosing a set of edges
E
and taking the
P_{\jmath}
to be the connected components of
{\mathrm{Tr}}_{{\bm{\sigma}}_{\setminus E}}(\vec{Q})
(which rewrites in the pure case as
{\mathrm{Tr}}_{{\bm{\sigma}}_{\setminus E}}(\vec{X},\vec{X}^{\prime})
),
one gets some
{\bm{\nu}}_{\jmath}
instead of
\mathsf{h}_{\jmath}
, and the relations (
7.13
) and (
7.14
) become
{\mathrm{Tr}}_{{\bm{\nu}}_{\jmath}}(\vec{Q})={\mathrm{Tr}}(P_{\jmath})
and
{\mathrm{Tr}}_{\mathsf{{\bm{\sigma}}}}(\vec{Q})={\mathrm{Tr}}_{\mathsf{k}}(\vec{P})
.

Alternating and almost alternating invariants.

Given a trace-invariant of paired tensors
H_{1},\ldots H_{p}
encoded by its colored graph
\mathsf{g}
, with
H_{\ell}\in\mathcal{G}^{\mathrm{m}}_{D}[Q_{\ell}]
with
Q_{\ell}\in\{A,B\ldots\}
, or
H_{\ell}\in\mathcal{G}^{\mathrm{p}}_{D}[X_{\ell},\bar{X}_{\ell}]
, where
(X_{\ell},\bar{X}_{\ell})\in\{(T_{a},\bar{T}_{a}),(T_{b},\bar{T}_{b})\ldots\}
(that is, here, if
X_{\ell}=T_{a}
, one must have
\bar{X}_{\ell}=\bar{T}_{a}
, etc),
we will say that
(\mathsf{g},\vec{H})
is
alternating
or
strictly alternating
if it has more than one paired tensor and all the edges of color
1\leq c\leq D
of
\mathsf{g}
that link some
H_{\ell}
,
H_{\ell^{\prime}}
with
\ell\neq\ell^{\prime}
are such that
H_{\ell}
and
H_{\ell^{\prime}}
are generated by different tensors
Q_{\ell}\neq Q_{\ell^{\prime}}
or
(X_{\ell},\bar{X}_{\ell})\neq(X_{\ell^{\prime}},\bar{X}_{\ell^{\prime}})
. Note that any number of edges may link the inputs and outputs of a given
H_{\ell}
.

We say that
(\mathsf{g},\vec{H})
is
almost alternating
, if at least one edge of some color
1\leq c\leq D
links some paired tensors
H_{\ell}
,
H_{\ell^{\prime}}
with
\ell\neq\ell^{\prime}
and generated by different tensors
Q_{\ell}\neq Q_{\ell^{\prime}}
, and
at most one
edge links two paired tensors
H_{\ell}
,
H_{\ell^{\prime}}
with
\ell\neq\ell^{\prime}
and generated by the same
Q_{\ell}=Q_{\ell^{\prime}}
.

Figure 15:

Examples of alternating (left) and almost alternating (right) melonic
(\mathsf{g},\vec{H})
, where the letters indicate the generators for the paired tensors represented by the thick edges. In the pure case, the generators
A,B,C
are replaced by
(T_{a},\bar{T}_{a}),(T_{b},\bar{T}_{b}),(T_{c},\bar{T}_{c})
.

With the same notations, if
(\mathsf{g},\vec{H})
is not alternating, we may form groups of tensors to form coarser invariants which are alternating, as we now detail.
In
\mathsf{g}
, let
E^{\neq}=E^{\neq}(\mathsf{g},\vec{H})
be the set of edges of color
c\in\{1,\ldots D\}
that link paired tensors
H_{\ell},H_{\ell^{\prime}}

generated by different
tensors
Q_{\ell}\neq Q_{\ell^{\prime}}
36
36

36

For the example on the right of Fig.
15
,
E^{\neq}
is the set of crossed edges on the left of Fig.
14
.
. We choose
E^{\mathrm{int}}
a set of edges of
\mathsf{g}_{\setminus E^{\neq}}
, such that
E^{\mathrm{int}}
contains one edge per cycle of
\mathsf{g}_{\setminus E^{\neq}}
that alternates edges of color
c
and paired inputs and outputs, and let
E^{\mathrm{tot}}=E^{\neq}\cup E^{\mathrm{int}}
.
We let
P^{\neq}_{\jmath}=P^{\neq}_{\jmath}(\mathsf{g},\vec{H},E^{\mathrm{int}})
and
\mathsf{h}^{\neq}_{\jmath}=\mathsf{h}^{\neq}_{\jmath}(\mathsf{g},\vec{H},E^{\mathrm{int}})
be the
P_{\jmath}
and
\mathsf{h}_{\jmath}
of
\mathsf{g}_{\setminus E^{\mathrm{tot}}}
, constructed as above. From the construction, all paired tensors
H_{\ell}
that belong to the same
P^{\neq}_{\jmath}
are generated by the
same
tensor
Q_{\ell}=Q(\jmath)
in
\{A,B\ldots\}
, so that from (
7.12
), in the mixed case,
P^{\neq}_{\jmath}\in\mathcal{G}^{\mathrm{m}}_{D}[Q(\jmath)]
, and in the pure case,
Q(\jmath)=X(\jmath)\otimes\bar{X}(\jmath)
and
P^{\neq}_{\jmath}\in\mathcal{G}^{\mathrm{p}}_{D}[X(\jmath),\bar{X}(\jmath)]
. The colored graph
\mathsf{k}
built above is in the present situation denoted by
\mathsf{k}^{\neq}=\mathsf{k}^{\neq}(\mathsf{g},\vec{H},E^{\mathrm{int}})
. From the construction, every edge
e
of
\mathsf{k}^{\neq}
either links paired tensors
P^{\neq}_{\jmath},P^{\neq}_{\jmath^{\prime}}
with
\jmath\neq\jmath^{\prime}
that are generated by different tensors
Q(\jmath)\neq Q(\jmath^{\prime})
, or
e
links and input and output of one paired tensor
P^{\neq}_{\jmath}
:
(\mathsf{k}^{\neq},\vec{P}^{\neq})
is strictly alternating.

7.5
Asymptotic moments of random paired tensors

Asymptotics.

Consider any paired-tensors
H_{1},\ldots H_{q}
,
H_{\ell}\in\mathcal{G}^{\mathrm{m}}_{D}[S_{\ell}]
, where
S_{\ell}\subset\{A,B\ldots\}
, or
H_{\ell}\in\mathcal{G}^{\mathrm{p}}_{D}[\Theta_{\ell},\bar{\Theta}_{\ell}]
, where
\Theta_{\ell}\subset\{T_{a},T_{b}\ldots\}
and
\bar{\Theta}_{\ell}\subset\{\bar{T}_{a},\bar{T}_{b}\ldots\}
, and a trace-invariant of these
q
paired tensors with colored graph
\mathsf{g}
, that involves a total of
n
regular tensors
M_{1},\ldots M_{n}
,
M_{i}\in\{A,B\ldots\}
or
M_{i}=X_{i}\otimes X_{\bar{i}}^{\prime}
with with
X_{i}\in\{T_{a},T_{b}\ldots\}
and
X_{\bar{i}}^{\prime}\in\{\bar{T}_{a},\bar{T}_{b}\ldots\}
. Then from (
7.9
), there is a
{\bm{\tau}}\in S_{n}^{D}
such that
{\mathrm{Tr}}_{\mathsf{g}}(\vec{H})={\mathrm{Tr}}_{\bm{\tau}}(\vec{M})
.
Furthermore,
\mathsf{g}
is a connected melonic graph of paired tensors if and only if
{\bm{\tau}}
is
first order (Lemma
7.9
).
Assume that the regular random tensors
\{A,B\ldots\}
or
\{T_{a},\bar{T}_{a},T_{b},\bar{T}_{b}\ldots\}
scale as assumed in the previous sections respectively in the mixed (
6.37
)
and (
6.38
) and pure (
6.10
) and (
6.15
) cases.
In both cases, the expectations of trace-invariants for

first order

{\bm{\tau}}
are
of order
N
, and we may set:

\phi_{\mathsf{g}}\bigl(\vec{h}\bigr):=\lim_{N\rightarrow\infty}\frac{1}{N}\mathbb{E}\bigl[{\mathrm{Tr}}_{{\bm{\tau}}}(\vec{M})\bigr].

(7.15)

In the mixed and pure case, this respectively corresponds to the dedicated notations:

\phi_{\mathsf{g}}(\vec{h})=\varphi_{{\bm{\tau}}}^{\mathrm{m}}(\vec{m})\qquad\textrm{ or }\qquad\phi_{\mathsf{g}}(\vec{h})=\varphi_{{\bm{\tau}}}(\vec{x},\vec{x^{\prime}}).

(7.16)

We say that
(\mathsf{g},\vec{h})
is
almost or strictly alternating
if
(\mathsf{g},\vec{H})
is.

Considering a set
E
of edges of
\mathsf{g}
satisfying the condition
above (
7.12
)
and splitting open the edges of
E
defines as in (
7.12
) some
P_{\jmath}\in\mathcal{G}^{\mathrm{m}}_{D}[S_{\jmath}]
or
\mathcal{G}^{\mathrm{p}}_{D}[\Theta_{\jmath},\bar{\Theta}_{\jmath}]
, and with the notations of Sec.
7.4
, some
\vec{H}_{\jmath}
,
{\bm{\tau}}_{\jmath}
, and
\mathsf{k}
. The
{\bm{\tau}}_{\jmath}
being
first order, we may define as in (
7.15
)

\phi_{\mathsf{h}_{\jmath}}(\vec{h}_{\jmath})=\varphi_{{\bm{\tau}}_{\jmath}}^{\mathrm{m}}(\vec{m}_{\jmath})
, or in the pure case
\phi_{\mathsf{h}_{\jmath}}(\vec{h}_{\jmath})=\varphi_{{\bm{\tau}}_{\jmath}}(\vec{x}_{\jmath},\vec{{x}_{\jmath}^{\prime}})
, so that (
7.13
) becomes:

\phi_{\mathsf{h}_{\jmath}}(\vec{h}_{\jmath})=\phi_{\mathbf{id}_{1}}(p_{\jmath})=\phi(p_{\jmath}).

(7.17)

Asymptotically, (
7.14
) implies:

\phi_{\mathsf{g}}(\vec{h})=\phi_{\mathsf{k}}(\vec{p}).

(7.18)

In particular, starting from
{\bm{\sigma}}\in S_{n}^{D}
purely connected with
\bar{\omega}({\bm{\sigma}};\mathrm{id})=0
and
\vec{Q}\in\{A,B\ldots\}
(or
Q_{i}=X_{i}\otimes X_{\bar{i}}^{\prime}
with
X_{i}\in\{T_{a},T_{b}\ldots\}
and
X_{\bar{i}}^{\prime}\in\{\bar{T}_{a},\bar{T}_{b}\ldots\}
) instead of
\vec{H}
, with
{\bm{\nu}}_{\jmath}
instead of
\mathsf{h}_{\jmath}
:
\varphi_{{\bm{\nu}}_{\jmath}}^{\mathrm{m}}(\vec{q})=\phi(p_{\jmath})
and
\varphi^{\mathrm{m}}_{{\bm{\sigma}}}(\vec{q})=\phi_{\mathsf{k}}(\vec{p})
(or pure equivalent
\varphi_{{\bm{\nu}}_{\jmath}}(\vec{x},\vec{x^{\prime}})=\phi(p_{\jmath})
and
\varphi_{{\bm{\sigma}}}(\vec{x},\vec{x^{\prime}})=\phi_{\mathsf{k}}(\vec{p})
).

Multilinearity.

We now consider the asymptotics of
{\mathrm{Tr}}_{\mathsf{g}}(\vec{H}^{\prime\prime})
, where
H^{\prime\prime}_{\ell}=H_{\ell}+t_{\ell}H^{\prime}_{\ell}
with
t_{\ell}\in\mathbb{C}
and
H_{\ell},H^{\prime}_{\ell}\in\mathcal{G}^{\mathrm{m}}_{D}[S]
with
S=\{A,B\ldots\}
, or
H_{\ell}\in\mathcal{G}^{\mathrm{p}}_{D}[\Theta,\bar{\Theta}]
, where
\Theta=\{T_{a},T_{b}\ldots\}
and
\bar{\Theta}=\{\bar{T}_{a},\bar{T}_{b}\ldots\}
,
\mathsf{g}
a connected melonic graph of these paired tensors. By multilinearity:

{\mathrm{Tr}}_{\mathsf{g}}(\vec{H}^{\prime\prime})=\sum_{k=0}^{p}\ \sum_{1\leq\ell(1)<\ell(2)<\ldots<\ell(k)\leq p}\Bigl(\prod_{j=1}^{k}t_{\ell(j)}\Bigr)\;{\mathrm{Tr}}_{\mathsf{g}}\Bigl(\vec{H}^{\prime\prime}_{[\ell(1),\ldots,\ell(k)]}\Bigr),

(7.19)

where setting
I=\{\ell(1),\ldots,\ell(k)\}
, the
\ell
th component of
\vec{H}^{\prime\prime}_{[I]}
is
H_{\ell}
if
\ell\notin I
and is
H_{\ell}^{\prime}
otherwise. Due to (
7.9
), (
6.15
) and (
6.38
),

{\mathrm{Tr}}_{\mathsf{g}}(\vec{H}^{\prime\prime}_{[I]})
is of order
N
and the limit of the rescaled expectation is:

\phi_{\mathsf{g}}(\vec{h}^{\prime\prime})=\sum_{k=0}^{p}\ \sum_{1\leq\ell(1)<\ell(2)<\ldots<\ell(k)\leq p}\Bigl(\prod_{j=1}^{k}t_{\ell(j)}\Bigr)\;\phi_{\mathsf{g}}\Bigl(\vec{h}^{\prime\prime}_{[I)]}\Bigr),\qquad h^{\prime\prime}_{\ell}=h_{\ell}+t_{\ell}h^{\prime}_{\ell}\;,

(7.20)

where the
\ell
th component of
\vec{h}^{\prime\prime}_{[I]}
is
h_{\ell}
if
\ell\notin I
and
h_{\ell}^{\prime}
otherwise:
\phi_{\mathsf{g}}
is multilinear by construction.

7.6
Asymptotically centering paired tensors

For any
\mathcal{D}\in\mathbb{N}^{\star}
, we let:

\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{D}}=\frac{\mathbbm{1}^{\otimes\mathcal{D}}}{N^{\mathcal{D}-1}}.

(7.21)

If
H\in\mathcal{G}^{\mathrm{m}}_{D,\mathcal{D}}[S]
with
S=\{A,B\ldots\}
or
H\in\mathcal{G}^{\mathrm{p}}_{D,\mathcal{D}}[\Theta,\bar{\Theta}]
, where
\Theta=\{T_{a},T_{b}\ldots\}
and
\bar{\Theta}=\{\bar{T}_{a},\bar{T}_{b}\ldots\}
, in order to asymptotically center
H
, we may define at finite
N
:

G=H-\phi(h)\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{D}},

(7.22)

where
\phi(h)=\phi_{\mathbf{id}_{1}}(h)
, so if
H
involves a total of
n
regular tensors
M_{1},\ldots M_{n}
,
M_{i}\in\{A,B\ldots\}
or
M_{i}=X_{i}\otimes X_{\bar{i}}^{\prime}
with
X_{i}\in\{T_{a},T_{b}\ldots\}
and
X_{\bar{i}}^{\prime}\in\{\bar{T}_{a},\bar{T}_{b}\ldots\}
, there exists a first order
{\bm{\sigma}}\in S_{n}^{D}
such that
\phi(h)=\varphi_{\bm{\sigma}}^{\mathrm{m}}(\vec{m})
in the mixed case and
\phi(h)=\varphi_{\bm{\sigma}}(\vec{x},\vec{x^{\prime}})
in the pure case.

We need to justify that the rescaling (
7.22
) is the appropriate one for considering quantities
like
\phi_{\mathsf{g}}
, that it will be asymptotically multilinear, and that
G
thus defined will be asymptotically centered:
\phi(g)=0
.
The reason why the identity is rescaled by
N^{\mathcal{D}-1}
is because for any
{\bm{\sigma}}\in S_{n}^{D}
:

{\mathrm{Tr}}_{\bm{\sigma}}(\mathbbm{1}_{D})=N^{n-d({\bm{\sigma}},\mathrm{id})}=N^{1-(D-1)(K_{\mathrm{p}}({\bm{\sigma}})-1)-\bar{\omega}({\bm{\sigma}};\mathrm{id})},

(7.23)

so if
{\bm{\sigma}}
is purely connected and
\bar{\omega}({\bm{\sigma}};\mathrm{id})=0
one has
{\mathrm{Tr}}_{\bm{\sigma}}(\mathbbm{1}_{D})=N
.

Lemma 7.2
.

Any element of
\mathcal{G}^{\mathrm{m}}_{D,\mathcal{D}}[\mathbbm{1}_{D}]

which originates from a purely connected
{\bm{\sigma}}
is equal to
\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{D}}
. Therefore for any
\mathcal{D}
there exists
{\bm{\sigma}}\in S_{n}^{D}
with
n=(\mathcal{D}-1)/(n-1)
,
{\bm{\sigma}}
purely connected with
\bar{\omega}({\bm{\sigma}};\mathrm{id})=0
, and a set
E
consisting of
\mathcal{D}
edges, one per cycle alternating
edges of color
c
and thick edges, such that:

\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{D}}={\mathrm{Tr}}_{{\bm{\sigma}}_{\setminus_{E}}}\left(\mathbbm{1}_{D}\right)

(7.24)

Proof.

With the notations of the lemma, every path of
{\bm{\sigma}}
consisting alternatively of thick edges representing and edges of color
c
and whose extremities are half-edges represents a Kronnecker delta in the corresponding indices. The cycles of this kind contribute as factors of
N
, and the overall exponent of
N
is
n-d({\bm{\sigma}},\mathrm{id})-\mathcal{D}=1-\mathcal{D}
. ∎

If
\mathsf{g}
is a connected melonic graph of
\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{D}_{1}},\ldots\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{D}_{p}}
, then from Lemma
7.2
, (
7.9
) and (
7.23
):

{\mathrm{Tr}}_{\mathsf{g}}\left(\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{D}_{1}},\ldots,\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{D}_{p}}\right)=N,

(7.25)

leading to the same global rescaling
N
for melonic invariants as in
(
7.15
). By linearity of
{\mathrm{Tr}}={\mathrm{Tr}}_{\mathbf{id}_{1}}
, we do find that
G
introduced in (
7.22
) satisfies
\phi(g)=\phi_{\mathbf{id}_{1}}(g)=0
.

Lemma
7.2
also implies that the following sets both contain
\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{D}}
:

•

The set
\mathcal{G}^{\mathrm{p}}_{D,\mathcal{D}}[\Theta,\bar{\Theta};\mathbbm{1}_{D}]
of paired tensors with
\mathcal{D}
inputs obtained from pure first order invariants (Sec.
7.3
, see also Fig.
12
), where any thick edge may represent
\mathbbm{1}_{D}
, or any of the elements of the form
X_{i}\otimes X_{\bar{i}}^{\prime}
with
X_{i}\in\Theta
and
X_{\bar{i}}^{\prime}\in\bar{\Theta}
.

•

The set
\mathcal{G}^{\mathrm{m}}_{D,\mathcal{D}}[S;\mathbbm{1}_{D}]
of paired tensors with
\mathcal{D}
inputs obtained from mixed first order invariants, where any thick edge may represent any element of
S
, but only thick edge that coincide with a canonical pair may be replaced with
\mathbbm{1}_{D}
.

Respectively let
\mathcal{A}^{\mathrm{m}}_{D,\mathcal{D}}[S;\mathbbm{1}_{D}]
and
\mathcal{A}^{\mathrm{p}}_{D,\mathcal{D}}[\Theta,\bar{\Theta};\mathbbm{1}_{D}]
be the sets of complex linear combinations of elements of
\mathcal{G}^{\mathrm{m}}_{D,\mathcal{D}}[S;\mathbbm{1}_{D}]
and
\mathcal{G}^{\mathrm{p}}_{D,\mathcal{D}}[\theta,\bar{\theta};\mathbbm{1}_{D}]
. (
7.22
) is an element of
\mathcal{A}^{\mathrm{m}}_{D,\mathcal{D}}[S;\mathbbm{1}_{D}]
in the mixed case and of
\mathcal{A}^{\mathrm{p}}_{D,\mathcal{D}}[\Theta,\bar{\Theta};\mathbbm{1}_{D}]
in the pure case. We let
\mathcal{A}^{\mathrm{m}}_{D}=\cup_{\mathcal{D}\geq 1}\mathcal{A}^{\mathrm{m}}_{D,\mathcal{D}}
and
\mathcal{A}^{\mathrm{p}}_{D}=\cup_{\mathcal{D}\geq 1}\mathcal{A}^{\mathrm{p}}_{D,\mathcal{D}}
. For
D=1
and
S=\{A\}
,
\mathcal{A}^{\mathrm{m}}_{1}[A;\mathbbm{1}]=\mathcal{A}^{\mathrm{m}}_{1,1}[A;\mathbbm{1}]
is the algebra
\mathcal{A}[A,\mathbbm{1}]
generated by
\mathbbm{1}
and
A
.

Considering
\vec{H}^{\prime}
where
H^{\prime}_{\ell}=H_{\ell}+\alpha_{\ell}\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{D}_{\ell}}
where
H_{\ell}\in\mathcal{G}^{\mathrm{m}}_{D,\mathcal{D}_{\ell}}[S_{\ell}]
,
S_{\ell}\subset\{A,B\ldots\}
, or
H_{\ell}\in\mathcal{G}^{\mathrm{p}}_{D,\mathcal{D}_{\ell}}[\Theta_{\ell},\bar{\Theta}_{\ell}]
, where
\Theta_{\ell}\subset\{T_{a},T_{b}\ldots\}
and
\bar{\Theta}_{\ell}\subset\{\bar{T}_{a},\bar{T}_{b}\ldots\}
, in order to make sense of
\phi_{\mathsf{g}}\bigl(\vec{h}^{\prime}\bigr)
, where
\mathsf{g}
is a connected melonic graph of
p
paired tensors, we write by multilinearity:

{\mathrm{Tr}}_{\mathsf{g}}(\vec{H}^{\prime})=\sum_{k=0}^{p}\ \sum_{1\leq\ell(1)<\ell(2)<\ldots<\ell(k)\leq p}\Bigl(\prod_{j=1}^{k}\alpha_{\ell(j)}\Bigr)\;{\mathrm{Tr}}_{\mathsf{g}}\Bigl(\vec{H}_{[\ell(1),\ldots,\ell(k)]}\Bigr),

(7.26)

where letting
I=\{\ell(1),\ldots,\ell(k)\}
, the component of index
\ell
of the vector
\vec{H}_{[I]}
is
H_{\ell}
if
\ell\notin I
and is
\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{D}_{\ell}}
otherwise. We therefore need to study the asymptotics of the right-hand-side.

Figure 16:
Removal of a thick edge (
D=4
,
l=2
) from a purely connected melonic graph
{\bm{\sigma}}
with
\bar{\omega}({\bm{\sigma}};\mathrm{id})=0
. The grey blobs represent parts of the graph which are disconnected from one another due to the properties of
{\bm{\sigma}}
, so that there are two connected components on the right.

If
{\bm{\sigma}}\in S_{n}^{D}
is purely connected and
\bar{\omega}({\bm{\sigma}};\mathrm{id})=0
, choosing a thick edge labeled
i
, we may remove this thick edge and the two vertices it links from the graph, as well as the
D-l
colored edges that link these two vertices, and then reconnect the
l
pending half-edges of color
1,\ldots D
as in Fig.
16
. Permutation-wise, this corresponds to removing
i
from all cycles of the permutations
\sigma_{c}
, leading to a new
D
-tuple
{\bm{\sigma}}_{\setminus i}
. This invariant is still melonic with
\bar{\omega}({\bm{\sigma}};\mathrm{id})=0
, but it now has
l
(purely) connected components. One has for any
\vec{M}
with
37
37

37

The result does not depend on which
M_{i}
is set to
\mathbbm{1}_{D}
.

M_{n}=\mathbbm{1}_{D}
:

{\mathrm{Tr}}_{{\bm{\sigma}}}\bigl(\vec{M}\bigr)=\frac{1}{N^{l-1}}{\mathrm{Tr}}_{{\bm{\sigma}}_{\setminus n}}\bigl(\vec{M}_{\setminus n})\bigr),

(7.27)

where
l-1=D-1+l-D
. In the pure case,
\vec{M}
has components
M_{i}=X_{i}\otimes X_{\bar{i}}^{\prime}
. Since both sides are first order, the expectation of the left-hand side is of order
N
and that of the trace-invariant on the right-hand side is of order
l
, so that in the end we find (here
\Pi({\bm{\sigma}}_{\setminus n})=\Pi_{\mathrm{p}}({\bm{\sigma}}_{\setminus n})
):

\phi_{{\bm{\sigma}}}\bigl(m_{1},\ldots,m_{n-1},1_{D}\bigr):=\lim_{N}\mathbb{E}\biggl[\frac{{\mathrm{Tr}}_{{\bm{\sigma}}}\bigl(\vec{M}\bigr)}{N}\biggr]=\lim_{N}\mathbb{E}\biggl[\frac{{\mathrm{Tr}}_{{\bm{\sigma}}_{\setminus n}}\bigl(\vec{M}_{\setminus n})\bigr)}{{N^{l}}}\biggr]=\varphi^{\mathrm{m}}_{\Pi({\bm{\sigma}}_{\setminus n}),{\bm{\sigma}}_{\setminus n}}(m_{1},\ldots,m_{n-1}),

where for the pure case the right hand side can be replaced by
\varphi_{\Pi_{\mathrm{p}}({\bm{\sigma}}_{\setminus n}),{\bm{\sigma}}_{\setminus n}}(\vec{x}_{\setminus n},\vec{x^{\prime}}_{\setminus n})
.
One may iterate this process for all the thick edges whose labels are in
I=\{n-k+1,\ldots,n\}
, and as the procedure does not depend on the ordering of the thick edge removals, one may denote the resulting invariant by
{\bm{\sigma}}_{\setminus I}
. We may then define
\phi_{{\bm{\sigma}}}\bigl(m_{1},\ldots,m_{n-k},1_{D},\ldots,1_{D}\bigr)
in the same manner, and notice that for any
k<n
:

\phi_{{\bm{\sigma}}}\bigl(m_{1},\ldots,m_{n-k},1_{D},\ldots,1_{D}\bigr)=\varphi^{\mathrm{m}}_{\Pi({\bm{\sigma}}_{\setminus I}),{\bm{\sigma}}_{\setminus I}}(m_{1},\ldots,m_{n-k}),

or
\varphi_{\Pi_{\mathrm{p}}({\bm{\sigma}}_{\setminus I}),{\bm{\sigma}}_{\setminus I}}(\vec{x}_{\setminus I},\vec{x^{\prime}}_{\setminus I})
for the pure case. For
k=n
, one has from (
7.23
) that
\phi_{{\bm{\sigma}}}(1_{D})=1
.

If now
\mathsf{g}
is a more general connected melonic graph of
p
paired tensors
, and with the notations of (
7.26
), combining (
7.9
) and Lemma
7.1
for the
H_{\ell}
for
\ell\notin I
as well as Lemma
7.2
for the
\ell\in I
, we see that there exists a first order
{\bm{\sigma}}\in S_{n}^{D}
and
\vec{M}=(M_{1},\ldots,M_{n})
such that
{\mathrm{Tr}}_{\mathsf{g}}(\vec{H}_{[I]})={\mathrm{Tr}}_{\bm{\sigma}}(\vec{M}),
where
I=\{\ell(1),\ldots,\ell(k)\}
.
Each
M_{i}
comes from one of the components of
\vec{H}_{[I]}
, so it takes value in the set
\{\mathbbm{1}_{D},A,B\ldots\}
(in the pure case, each
M_{i}
different from
\mathbbm{1}_{D}
writes as
M_{i}=X_{i}\otimes X_{\bar{i}}^{\prime}
). We therefore define

\phi_{\mathsf{g}}(\vec{h}_{[I]})=\lim_{N\rightarrow\infty}\mathbb{E}[{\mathrm{Tr}}_{\bm{\sigma}}(\vec{M})]/N
, where the component of index
\ell
of
\vec{h}_{[I]}
is
h_{\ell}
if
\ell\notin\{\ell(1),\ldots,\ell(k)\}
and is
1_{\mathcal{D}_{\ell}}
otherwise. If
k=p
, we define
\phi_{\mathsf{g}}(1_{\mathcal{D}_{1}},\ldots,1_{\mathcal{D}_{p}})
as the limit of (
7.25
) rescaled by
1/N
, which is always equal to 1 regardless of the
\mathcal{D}_{\ell}
:

\phi_{\mathsf{g}}(1_{\mathcal{D}_{1}},\ldots,1_{\mathcal{D}_{p}})=1.

(7.28)

We define as above
\mathsf{g}_{\setminus I}
as the (non-necessarily connected) melonic graph of paired tensors obtained by removing the thick edges with indices in
I=\{\ell(1),\ldots,\ell(k)\}
from the graph in the same way as in Fig.
16
(the procedure can be adapted here since the inputs and outputs of the same color are paired). We also denote by
\phi_{\Pi(\mathsf{g}_{\setminus I}),\mathsf{g}_{\setminus I}}(\vec{h}_{\setminus I})
the product of the
\phi_{\mathsf{g}_{j}}
over the connected components
\mathsf{g}_{j}
of
\mathsf{g}_{\setminus I}
, where
\vec{h}_{\setminus I}
gathers the
h_{\ell}
for
\ell\notin I=\{\ell(1),\ldots,\ell(k)\}
. One has:

\phi_{\mathsf{g}}\bigl(\vec{h}_{[I]}\bigr)=\phi_{\Pi(\mathsf{g}_{\setminus I}),\mathsf{g}_{\setminus I}}(\vec{h}_{\setminus I}).

(7.29)

Since none of the arguments onn the right-hand side is 1, it can be expressed in terms of asymptotic moments of
\vec{m}
or
\vec{x},\vec{x^{\prime}}
. Finally coming back to (
7.26
), we see that all the terms in the sum are of order
N
, so we may rescale and take the limit of the expectation, to obtain:

\phi_{\mathsf{g}}(\vec{h}^{\prime})=\sum_{k=0}^{p}\ \sum_{1\leq\ell(1)<\ell(2)<\ldots<\ell(k)\leq p}\Bigl(\prod_{j=1}^{k}\alpha_{\ell(j)}\Bigr)\;\phi_{\mathsf{g}}\bigl(\vec{h}_{[I]}\bigr),\qquad h^{\prime}_{\ell}=h_{\ell}+\alpha_{\ell}1_{\mathcal{D}_{\ell}}

(7.30)

The notation
h^{\prime}_{\ell}=h_{\ell}+\alpha_{\ell}1_{\mathcal{D}_{\ell}}
is consistent with multilinearity, since the component of index
\ell
of
\vec{h}_{[I]}
is
h_{\ell}
if
\ell\notin\{\ell(1),\ldots,\ell(k)\}
and
1_{\mathcal{D}_{\ell}}
otherwise.

7.7
Free cumulants of paired tensors

We are now able to introduce free cumulants associated to connected melonic graphs of paired tensors. In Sec.
7.3
, we detailed how trace-invariants of paired tensors can be encoded by graphs
\mathsf{g}
or cycles
\{\gamma_{c,b}\}
, each of the form
\bigl((k_{1},r_{1})\cdots(k_{q_{c,b}},r_{q_{c,b}})\bigr)
where
1\leq k_{1},\ldots k_{q}\leq n
are the labels of the thick edges encountered in the cycle, and
r_{s}
is the shade of the pair of output and input of color
c
of the paired tensor number
k_{s}
which is in the cycle.

Consider any paired-tensors
H_{1},\ldots H_{q}
,
H_{\ell}\in\mathcal{G}^{\mathrm{m}}_{D}[S_{\ell}]
, where
S_{\ell}\subset\{A,B\ldots\}
, or
H_{\ell}\in\mathcal{G}^{\mathrm{p}}_{D}[\Theta_{\ell},\bar{\Theta}_{\ell}]
, where
\Theta_{\ell}\subset\{T_{a},T_{b}\ldots\}
and
\bar{\Theta}_{\ell}\subset\{\bar{T}_{a},\bar{T}_{b}\ldots\}
, and a trace-invariant of these
q
paired tensors with colored graph
\mathsf{g}
, encoded by some such cycles
\{\gamma_{c,b}\}
. We may independently consider some non-crossing
permutations

\{\tau_{c,b}\}
with
\tau_{c,b}\preceq\gamma_{c,b}
in
S_{\mathrm{NC}}(\gamma_{c,b})
, which is isomorphic to
\mathrm{NC}(q_{c,b})
, where
q_{c,b}
is the number of elements in the cycle
\gamma_{c,b}
.
We use the notation
\{\mathsf{h}\mid\mathsf{h}\preceq\mathsf{g}\}
to refer to the set of
\mathsf{h}
built from all the
\tau_{c,b}\preceq\gamma_{c,b}
, and the notation
\mathsf{M}(\mathsf{h},\mathsf{g})=\prod_{c,b}\mathsf{M}(\tau_{c,b}\gamma_{c,b}^{-1})
.

We may therefore define free cumulants for connected melonic graphs
\mathsf{g}
of
q
paired tensors for any
\vec{h}=(h_{1},\ldots h_{q})
as:

\varkappa_{\mathsf{g}}(\vec{h})=\sum_{\mathsf{h}\preceq\mathsf{g}}\phi_{\Pi(\mathsf{h}),\mathsf{h}}(\vec{h})\;\mathsf{M}(\mathsf{h},\mathsf{g}),

(7.31)

where as above,
\phi_{\Pi(\mathsf{h}),\mathsf{h}}(\vec{h})
is the product of the
\phi_{\mathsf{h}_{j}}
over the connected components
\mathsf{h}_{j}
of
\mathsf{h}
. By multilinearity of the
\phi
, the
\varkappa
are multilinear.

To understand this formula better, one can use (
7.16
) and Lemma
7.1
: we may gather the colored edges of
\mathsf{g}
together with the colored edges of each
H_{\ell}
, to form a colored graph of
M_{1},\ldots M_{n}
, namely, a first order invariant
{\bm{\sigma}}\in S_{n}^{D}
, such that
{\mathrm{Tr}}_{\mathsf{g}}(\vec{H})={\mathrm{Tr}}_{\bm{\sigma}}(\vec{M})
. In the mixed case,
M_{i}\in\{A,B\ldots\}
, while in the pure case,
M_{i}=X_{i}\otimes X_{\bar{i}}^{\prime}
with
X_{i}\in\{T_{a},T_{b}\ldots\}
and
X_{\bar{i}}^{\prime}\in\{\bar{T}_{a},\bar{T}_{b}\ldots\}
(so
{\mathrm{Tr}}_{\bm{\sigma}}(\vec{M})={\mathrm{Tr}}_{\bm{\sigma}}(\vec{X},\vec{X}^{\prime})
).

To each
\mathsf{h}\preceq\mathsf{g}
corresponds a
{\bm{\rho}}\in S_{n}^{D}
which satisfies
{\bm{\rho}}\eta^{-1}\preceq{\bm{\sigma}}\eta^{-1}
, where
\eta\in S_{n}
is the canonical pairing of
{\bm{\sigma}}
(pure) or
({\bm{\sigma}},\mathrm{id})
(mixed), but not any such
{\bm{\rho}}
may occur in the sum, as the edges internal to the
H_{\ell}
must be the same in
{\bm{\rho}}
and
{\bm{\sigma}}
, as will be detailed in the proof of Prop.
7.4
, see (
7.36
). One has:

\phi_{\Pi(\mathsf{h}),\mathsf{h}}(\vec{h})=\varphi^{\mathrm{m}}_{\Pi({\bm{\rho}}),{\bm{\rho}}}(\vec{m})\quad\textrm{(mixed)},\qquad\textrm{and}\qquad\phi_{\Pi(\mathsf{h}),\mathsf{h}}(\vec{h})=\varphi_{\Pi_{\mathrm{p}}({\bm{\rho}}),{\bm{\rho}}}(\vec{x},\vec{x^{\prime}})\quad\textrm{(pure)},

(7.32)

where in the pure case
\Pi({\bm{\rho}})=\Pi_{\mathrm{p}}({\bm{\rho}})
due to the fact that the connected components of
{\bm{\rho}}
are purely connected (since in that case the canonical pairing is the identity).

If
\mathsf{g}
is not necessarily connected, we set
\varkappa_{\Pi(\mathsf{g}),\mathsf{g}}(\vec{h})
to be the product of the
\varkappa_{\mathsf{g}_{i}}
over the connected components
\mathsf{g}_{i}
. We can invert the formula in the lattice
\bigtimes_{c,b}S_{\mathrm{NC}}(\gamma_{c,b})\cong\bigtimes_{c,b}\mathrm{NC}(q_{c,b})
, to obtain:

\phi_{\mathsf{g}}(\vec{h})=\sum_{\mathsf{h}\preceq\mathsf{g}}\varkappa_{\Pi(\mathsf{h}),\mathsf{h}}(\vec{h}).

(7.33)

The relation between
\varkappa
and
\kappa
is more complicated than the relation between
\phi
and
\varphi
(
7.32
): see (
7.34
) later in this section.
With the same notations as above, the following proposition, analogous to Prop. 11.15 of
[
1
]
. is proved in Sec.
C.1
.

Proposition 7.3
.

Let
\mathsf{g}
be a connected melonic graph of
q\geq 2
paired tensors
H_{1},\ldots H_{q}
,
\mathcal{D}_{\ell}
be the number of inputs of
H_{\ell}
, then one has

\varkappa_{\mathsf{g}}(\vec{h})=0

if one of the
h_{\ell}
is
1_{\mathcal{D}_{\ell}}
(i.e. if
H_{\ell}=\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{D}_{\ell}}
).

This of course applies as well to
\kappa_{\bm{\sigma}}(\vec{x},\vec{x^{\prime}})
and
\kappa^{\mathrm{m}}_{\bm{\sigma}}(\vec{m})
. The second result we will need relates free cumulants of paired tensors
\vec{H}
and those obtained by grouping them to obtain bigger paired tensors
\vec{P}
as in Sec.
7.4
.
See the proof in Sec.
C.1
. It generalizes Thm. 11.12 of
[
1
]
.

Proposition 7.4
.

Let
\mathsf{g}
be a connected melonic graph of
q
paired tensors
H_{1},\ldots H_{q}
,
H_{\ell}\in\mathcal{G}^{\mathrm{m}}_{D}[S_{\ell}]
, where
S_{\ell}\subset\{A,B\ldots\}
, or
H_{\ell}\in\mathcal{G}^{\mathrm{p}}_{D}[\Theta_{\ell},\bar{\Theta}_{\ell}]
, where
\Theta_{\ell}\subset\{T_{a},T_{b}\ldots\}
and
\bar{\Theta}_{\ell}\subset\{\bar{T}_{a},\bar{T}_{b}\ldots\}
,
E
a set of edges of
\mathsf{g}
, the
P_{\jmath}
obtained as connected components of
{\mathrm{Tr}}_{\mathsf{g}_{\setminus E}}(\vec{H})
,
the
\mathsf{h}_{\jmath}
such that

{\mathrm{Tr}}_{\mathsf{h}_{\jmath}}(\vec{H}_{\jmath})={\mathrm{Tr}}(P_{\jmath})
(
7.13
), and which constitute the connected components of
\mathsf{h}
, which is a melonic invariant of
\vec{H}
, and finally,
\mathsf{k}
such that
{\mathrm{Tr}}_{\mathsf{g}}(\vec{H})={\mathrm{Tr}}_{\mathsf{k}}(\vec{P})
(
7.14
). Then:

\varkappa_{\mathsf{k}}(\vec{p})=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}{\mathsf{h}^{\prime}\preceq\mathsf{g}}\\
{\Pi(\mathsf{h^{\prime}})\vee\Pi(\mathsf{h})=1_{q}}\end{subarray}}\varkappa_{\Pi(\mathsf{h}^{\prime}),\mathsf{h}^{\prime}}(\vec{h}),

where
\Pi(\mathsf{g})
,
\Pi(\mathsf{h})
are the partitions of
\{1,\ldots,q\}
induced by the connected components of
\mathsf{g}
,
\mathsf{h}
.

We may apply the proposition to a first order
{\bm{\sigma}}\in S_{n}^{D}
instead of the more general
\mathsf{g}
: in the mixed case, if
\eta\in S_{n}
is the canonical pairing of
({\bm{\sigma}},\mathrm{id})
,
\mathsf{k}
is a connected melonic graph of
q
paired tensors
P_{1},\ldots P_{q}
generated by
A,B\ldots
,
\vec{M}
is such that
{\mathrm{Tr}}_{\mathsf{k}}(\vec{P})={\mathrm{Tr}}_{\bm{\sigma}}(\vec{M})
(
7.9
),
{\bm{\tau}}_{\ell}
with support
R_{\ell}\subset\{1,\ldots n\}
is such that
{\mathrm{Tr}}(P_{\ell})={\mathrm{Tr}}_{{\bm{\tau}}_{\ell}}(\vec{M}_{\lvert B_{\ell}})
, and
{\bm{\tau}}\in S_{n}^{D}
has connected components
\{{\bm{\tau}}_{\ell}\}
:

\varkappa_{\mathsf{k}}(\vec{p})=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}{{\bm{\rho}}\eta^{-1}\preceq{\bm{\sigma}}\eta^{-1}}\\
{\Pi({\bm{\rho}}\eta^{-1})\vee\Pi({\bm{\tau}}\eta^{-1})=1_{n}}\end{subarray}}\kappa^{\mathrm{m}}_{\Pi({\bm{\rho}}),{\bm{\rho}}}(\vec{m}).

(7.34)

In the pure case, the
P_{1},\ldots P_{q}
are generated by
T_{a},T_{b}\ldots
and
\bar{T}_{a},\bar{T}_{b}\ldots
, one has
M_{i}=X_{i}\otimes X_{\bar{i}}^{\prime}
and
{\mathrm{Tr}}_{\mathsf{k}}(\vec{P})={\mathrm{Tr}}_{\bm{\sigma}}(\vec{X},\vec{X}^{\prime})
,
{\bm{\tau}}_{\ell}
is such that
{\mathrm{Tr}}(P_{\ell})={\mathrm{Tr}}_{{\bm{\tau}}_{\ell}}\bigl(\overrightarrow{X\otimes X^{\prime}}_{\lvert B_{\ell}}\bigr)
, and:

\varkappa_{\mathsf{k}}(\vec{p})=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}{{\bm{\rho}}\preceq{\bm{\sigma}}}\\
{\Pi({\bm{\rho}})\vee\Pi({\bm{\tau}})=1_{n}}\end{subarray}}\kappa_{\Pi_{\mathrm{p}}({\bm{\rho}}),{\bm{\rho}}}(\vec{x},\vec{x^{\prime}}).

(7.35)

The proof of Prop.
7.4
in Sec.
C.1
relies on the useful relation (
C.2
), which here simplifies to:

\displaystyle\varkappa_{\mathsf{k}}(\vec{p})

\displaystyle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}{{\bm{\rho}}\in S_{n}^{D}}\\
{{\bm{\tau}}\eta^{-1}\preceq{\bm{\rho}}\eta^{-1}\preceq{\bm{\sigma}}\eta^{-1}}\end{subarray}}\varphi^{\mathrm{m}}_{\Pi({\bm{\rho}}),{\bm{\rho}}}(\vec{m})

(mixed)

(7.36)

\displaystyle\varkappa_{\mathsf{k}}(\vec{p})

\displaystyle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}{{\bm{\rho}}\in S_{n}^{D}}\\
{{\bm{\tau}}\preceq{\bm{\rho}}\preceq{\bm{\sigma}}}\end{subarray}}\varphi_{\Pi({\bm{\rho}}),{\bm{\rho}}}(\vec{x},\vec{x^{\prime}})

(pure)

(7.37)

Consider now the situation where, in the mixed case,
\mathsf{k}
is a connected melonic graph of
P_{1},\ldots P_{q}
with
P_{\ell}
of the form (
7.7
), and
\vec{M}
such that
{\mathrm{Tr}}_{\mathsf{k}}(\vec{P})={\mathrm{Tr}}_{\bm{\sigma}}(\vec{M})
, so that
{\bm{\sigma}}\in S_{n}^{D}
is connected and
({\bm{\sigma}},\mathrm{id})
is melonic.
Then
{\bm{\tau}}
coincides with
(\eta,\eta,\ldots,\eta)
, so that in this particular situation:

\varkappa_{\mathsf{k}}(\vec{p})=\kappa^{\mathrm{m}}_{{\bm{\sigma}}}(\vec{m}).

(7.38)

7.8
Asymptotic tensor freeness at the level of moments

7.8.1
Mixed case

We consider some mixed random tensors
A,B\ldots
with
D\geq 2
inputs which scale as (
6.37
), and we consider the corresponding asymptotic moments and first order free cumulants
\varphi_{\bm{\sigma}}(\vec{m})
,
\kappa_{\bm{\sigma}}(\vec{m})
, where
\vec{m}\in\{a,b,\ldots\}^{n}
, as well as the asymptotic moments
\phi
and cumulants
\varkappa
associated to connected melonic invariants of paired tensors. The following is proved in Sec.
C.2.1
.

Theorem 7.5

(Asymptotic mixed tensor freeness)
.

The following statements are equivalent:

1.

For any
n\geq 2
, any
{\bm{\sigma}}\in S_{n}^{D}
connected and with
\omega({\bm{\sigma}},\mathrm{id})=0
, and any
\vec{m}=(m_{1},\ldots,m_{n})\in\{a,b,\ldots\}^{n}
,
\kappa^{\mathrm{m}}_{\bm{\sigma}}(\vec{m})=0
whenever there exists
1\leq i<j\leq n
such that
m_{i}\neq m_{j}
.

2.

The two following conditions are satisfied:

-

For any
n\geq 2
, any
{\bm{\sigma}}\in S_{n}^{D}
connected such that
({\bm{\sigma}},\mathrm{id})
melonic with canonical pairing
\eta\neq\mathrm{id}
, and any
\vec{m}=(m_{1},\ldots,m_{n})\in\{a,b,\ldots\}^{n}
,
\kappa^{\mathrm{m}}_{\bm{\sigma}}(\vec{m})=0
whenever there exists
i\in\{1,\ldots n\}
such that
m_{i}\neq m_{\eta(i)}
.

-

For any
q\geq 2
, any paired tensors
H_{1},\ldots H_{q}
such that
\forall\;1\leq\ell\leq q
,
H_{\ell}\in\mathcal{G}^{\mathrm{m}}_{D}[Q_{\ell}]
with
Q_{\ell}\in\{A,B\ldots\}
, and any connected melonic graph
\mathsf{g}
of the paired tensors
\vec{H}
,
\varkappa_{\mathsf{g}}(\vec{h})=0
whenever there exists
1\leq\ell<\ell^{\prime}\leq q
such that
Q_{\ell}\neq Q_{\ell^{\prime}}
.

3.

The two following conditions are satisfied:

-

For any
n\geq 2
, any
{\bm{\sigma}}\in S_{n}^{D}
connected and such that
({\bm{\sigma}},\mathrm{id})
is melonic with canonical pairing
\eta\neq\mathrm{id}
, and any
\vec{m}=(m_{1},\ldots,m_{n})\in\{a,b,\ldots\}^{n}
,
\varphi^{\mathrm{m}}_{\bm{\sigma}}(\vec{m})=0
whenever there exists
i\in\{1,\ldots n\}
such that
m_{i}\neq m_{\eta(i)}
.

-

For any
q\geq 2
, any
\mathcal{D}_{1},\ldots\mathcal{D}_{q}\geq 1
, any paired tensors
H_{1},\ldots H_{q}
such that for
1\leq\ell\leq q
,
H_{\ell}\in\mathcal{G}^{\mathrm{m}}_{D,\mathcal{D}_{\ell}}[Q_{\ell}]
with
Q_{\ell}\in\{A,B\ldots\}
, and any connected melonic graph
\mathsf{g}
of
H_{1},\ldots H_{q}
, letting for each
\ell
:

h^{\prime}_{\ell}=h_{\ell}-\phi(h_{\ell})1_{\mathcal{D}_{\ell}}
, then
\phi_{\mathsf{g}}(\vec{h}^{\prime})=0
whenever
(\mathsf{g},\vec{h})
is almost alternating.

Notice the dissymmetry: the first statements of point 2 and 3 do not require the notion of paired tensors nor introducing
\mathcal{G}^{\mathrm{m}}_{D}[Q_{\ell}]
. These notions are needed for the second statements of 2 and 3, because of the necessity to center the
h_{\ell}
. For point 3, for
A,B,C
asymptotically free, the example on the right of Fig.
13
with
H_{1},\ldots H_{4}
respectively generated by
A
,
B
,
C
and
C
is almost alternating (Fig.
15
) and therefore vanishes when the
H_{i}
are asymptotically centered.

For points 2 and 3, one can replace
\mathcal{G}^{\mathrm{m}}_{D,\mathcal{D}_{\ell}}[Q_{\ell}]
by
\mathcal{A}^{\mathrm{m}}_{D,\mathcal{D}_{\ell}}[Q_{\ell};\mathbbm{1}_{D}]
etc, see Rk.
C.2
and Rk.
C.6
.

7.8.2
Pure case

We now consider some pure random tensors
T_{a},\bar{T}_{a}
,
T_{b},\bar{T}_{b}\ldots
for
D\geq 3
, which scale as (
6.10
) and (
6.15
), and we consider the corresponding asymptotic moments and first order free cumulants
\varphi_{\bm{\sigma}}(\vec{x},\vec{x^{\prime}})
,
\kappa_{\bm{\sigma}}(\vec{x},\vec{x^{\prime}})
, where for each
i
,
x_{i}\in\{t_{a},t_{b}\ldots\}
and
x_{\bar{i}}^{\prime}\in\{\bar{t}_{a},\bar{t}_{b}\ldots\}
, as well as the
\phi
and
\varkappa
.

Theorem 7.6

(Asymptotic pure tensor freeness)
.

The following statements are equivalent:

1.

For any
n\geq 2
, any
{\bm{\sigma}}\in S_{n}^{D}
purely connected and melonic with canonical pairing the identity, and any
\vec{x}\in\{t_{a},t_{b}\ldots\}^{n}
and
\vec{x^{\prime}}\in\{\bar{t}_{a},\bar{t}_{b}\ldots\}^{n}
,
\kappa_{\bm{\sigma}}(\vec{x},\vec{x^{\prime}})=0
whenever
x_{i}\neq x_{j}
or
x_{\bar{i}}^{\prime}\neq x_{\bar{j}}^{\prime}
, or
\overline{x_{i}}\neq x_{\bar{j}}^{\prime}
for some
i,j
.

2.

The two following conditions are satisfied:

-

For any
n\geq 2
, any
{\bm{\sigma}}\in S_{n}^{D}
purely connected and melonic with canonical pairing the identity, and any
\vec{x}\in\{t_{a},t_{b}\ldots\}^{n}
and
\vec{x^{\prime}}\in\{\bar{t}_{a},\bar{t}_{b}\ldots\}^{n}
,
\kappa_{\bm{\sigma}}(\vec{x},\vec{x^{\prime}})=0
whenever there exists
i\in\{1,\ldots n\}
such that
\overline{x_{i}}\neq x_{\bar{i}}^{\prime}
.

-

For any
q\geq 2
, any paired tensors
H_{1},\ldots H_{q}
such that
\forall\;1\leq\ell\leq q
,
H_{\ell}\in\mathcal{G}^{\mathrm{p}}_{D}[X_{\ell},\bar{X}_{\ell}]
where
(X_{\ell},\bar{X}_{\ell})\in\{(T_{a},\bar{T}_{a}),(T_{b},\bar{T}_{b})\ldots\}
, and any connected melonic graph
\mathsf{g}
of the paired tensors
\vec{H}
,
\varkappa_{\mathsf{g}}(\vec{h})=0
whenever
\exists

1\leq\ell<\ell^{\prime}\leq q
such that
(X_{\ell},\bar{X}_{\ell})\neq(X_{\ell^{\prime}},\bar{X}_{\ell^{\prime}})
.

3.

The two following conditions are satisfied:

-

For any
n\geq 2
, any
{\bm{\sigma}}\in S_{n}^{D}
purely connected and melonic with canonical pairing the identity, and any
\vec{x}\in\{t_{a},t_{b}\ldots\}^{n}
and
\vec{x^{\prime}}\in\{\bar{t}_{a},\bar{t}_{b}\ldots\}^{n}
,
\varphi_{\bm{\sigma}}(\vec{x},\vec{x^{\prime}})=0
whenever there exists
i\in\{1,\ldots n\}
such that
\overline{x_{i}}\neq x_{\vec{i}}^{\prime}
.

-

For any
q\geq 2
, any
\mathcal{D}_{1},\ldots\mathcal{D}_{q}\geq 1
, any
H_{1},\ldots H_{q}
such that
\forall

1\leq\ell\leq q
,
H_{\ell}\in\mathcal{G}^{\mathrm{p}}_{D,\mathcal{D}_{\ell}}[X_{\ell},\bar{X}_{\ell}]
where
(X_{\ell},\bar{X}_{\ell})\in\{(T_{a},\bar{T}_{a}),(T_{b},\bar{T}_{b})\ldots\}
, and any connected melonic graph
\mathsf{g}
of
H_{1},\ldots H_{q}
, letting for each
\ell
:

h^{\prime}_{\ell}=h_{\ell}-\phi(h_{\ell})1_{\mathcal{D}_{\ell}}
, then
\phi_{\mathsf{g}}(\vec{h}^{\prime})=0
whenever
(\mathsf{g},\vec{h})
is almost alternating.

The proof can be found in Sec.
C.2.2
. For points 2 and 3, one can replace
H_{\ell}\in\mathcal{G}^{\mathrm{p}}_{D,\mathcal{D}_{\ell}}[X_{\ell},\bar{X}_{\ell}]
by
H_{\ell}\in\mathcal{A}^{\mathrm{m}}_{D,\mathcal{D}_{\ell}}[X_{\ell},\bar{X}_{\ell};\mathbbm{1}_{D}]
, see Rk.
C.2
and Rk.

and Thm.
8.2
.

For the second statement of point 3, for
T_{a},\bar{T}_{a}
,
T_{b},\bar{T}_{b}
,
T_{c},\bar{T}_{c}
asymptotically free, the example on the left of Fig.

, with

,

and

, vanishes when the
